yāṉ aimpatu āṇṭukaḷukku mēlāka ērāḷamāṉa nūlkaḷil ārāyntu kaṇṭa muṭivukaḷai nūlkaḷāka eḻutiyi rukkiṉṟēṉ.
pāṟkaṭalaik kaṭaintu amutam eṭuttatu pōla, uṇmai kāṇum uṇarcciyiṉāl naṭunilai niṉṟu ārāyntiruk kiṉṟēṉ. 
oruvar tam vāḻnāḷ muḻuvatum kaṟṟālum aṟiya muṭiyāta uṇmaikaḷai ellām eḷitāka eḻuti vait tirukkiṉṟēṉ.
ellārum tamiḻ ilakkiyaṅkaḷyāvaṟṟaiyum kaṟkum tollaiyai mēṟkoḷḷa vēṇṭiyatillai, atu muṭiyavum muṭiyātu, tēvaiyumillai. 
ellā nūlkaḷilum uḷḷa ciṟanta uṇmaikaḷai ellām piḻi cāṟāka yāṉ vaṭittut tantuḷḷēṉ.
eṉ nūlkaḷaip paṭittāṟpōtum, ataṉāl, tamiḻ muḻutuṅ kaṟṟa payaṉai aṭaiyalām.
- maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ



cāṉṟitaḻ
nākai - tiru. vētācalam piḷḷaiyavarkaḷ orupōtu tiruvaṉantapurattil oruvāran taṅkiyirunta ñāṉṟu, avaruṭaṉ neruṅkip paḻakum vāyppuk kiṭaittataṟkuc cālavum makiḻkiṉṟēm. varuṅkālat tamiḻ makkaḷukku maṭṭaṟṟa tamiḻppulamai mikuntu varumeṉpataṟkuc cāṉṟāka ivar iḷamaiyilēyē nuṇmāṇ nuḻaipulam uṭaiyarāyiruppatu kaḷippūṭṭukiṟatu. maṟṟum tāymoḻip paṟṟuk kuṟainta ikkālattil, ivar tamatu nuṇṇaṟivait tamiḻ nūlkaḷil muṟṟum celutti naṉkārāyntuḷḷatum pārāṭṭaṟkuriyatu. pattuppāṭṭu, kalittokai, cīvakacintāmaṇi mutaliya paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷil ārāycciyuṭaṉ kūṭiya ivartam aṟivuṭaimaiyē eṉṉai ivarpāl matippuṟumāṟu ceytatu. tamiḻāciriyarkaḷiṉ tokai curuṅkivarum ikkālattil ivarukkuc ciṟanta ūkkamaḷittāl ivar oru ciṟanta tamiḻāciriyarāvāreṉṟu nām uṟutiyāka nampukiṉṟēm. muṉṉarē iraṇṭāntarak kallūrikaḷil tamiḻkaṟpikkap pōtumāṉa aḷavu ilakkaṇa ilakkiyattiṟ ciṟanta tērcci peṟṟavarāyirukkiṉṟāreṉak karutukiṉṟōm. Fiwªj msÉš v~¥, V., (F.A.) vakuppukkuriya āṅkila aṟivu peṟṟārāṉāl mēlnāṭṭu muṟaippaṭi tamiḻ nūlkaḷai varalāṟṟuc cāṉṟuṭaṉ ārāyavum eḻutavum vallunarāvāreṉpatu emmuṭaiya ciṟanta eṇṇam.

2-12-1895
(inta naṟcāṉṟu tirunelvēli intuk kallūriyil cuntaram piḷḷaiyavarkaḷ pērāciriyarāka iruntapōtu koṭukkap peṟṟatu.)


tamiḻkkāvalar -  maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ
paṇṭait tamiḻ nūlkaḷaik kaṇṭavar eṉṟu cāmināta ayyaraiyum, tamiḻ nāṭakaṅ kaṇṭavareṉṟu cuntaram piḷḷaiyaiyum, caivan taḻaikka vanta vāṉmukil eṉṟu ñāṉiyāraṭikaḷaiyum, camayaṅkaḷiṉ camaracaṅ kaṇṭavareṉṟu tiru.vi. kaliyāṇacuntararaiyum, palkalai kaṇṭa aṟiñar eṉak kā.cuppiramaṇiya piḷḷaiyaiyum, kaviccuvai kaṇṭavareṉac citamparanātaraiyum, tamiḻk kalaikaḷ taḻaikka vanta pērāciriyar eṉṟu kalki (uh.kiUZz_®¤â)iaí«, pulattuṟai muṟṟiya nalattaku cāṉṟōreṉa vēṅkaṭacāmi nāṭṭāraiyum, tamiḻ oliyārāycciyiṉ nuṭpaṅ kaṇṭāreṉṟu māṇikka nāyakkaraiyum, ilakkiya nalaṅkaṇṭa ēntaleṉṟu katirēcaṉ ceṭṭiyāraiyum, vāravaḻipāṭukaṇṭa vaḷḷalār  eṉṟu caccitāṉantam piḷḷaiyaiyum, nāṭṭuṇarvūṭṭiya nallicaip pāvalareṉap pāratiyāraiyum pārāṭṭiya tamiḻulakam, irupatām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṇaiyillāp pulavarāṉa aṭikaḷait taṉittamiḻ kaṇṭa tamiḻk kāvalar eṉa uṇarcciyuṭaṉ pōṟṟukiṉṟatu. 
 - pērāciriyar maṟai. tirunāvukkaracu
maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ varalāṟu (pak 302)

maṟaimalaiya māṇpu
nuḻaivurai
taṉittamiḻ eṉṟa aḷavil muṉ niṟkum muḻuturu eḻilvaṭivar
tavattiru. maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷārē āvar!
muntai aṭikaḷ ākiya iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ, `muttamiḻ aṭikaḷ eṉa muḻakkamiṭa niṉṟār!
irupatām nūṟṟāṇṭu maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷō, taṉittamiḻt tantai eṉṉum takavārnta perumai peṟṟār.
tamiḻ taṉittu iyaṅkātu eṉṟum, vaṭamoḻi vaḻiyatu eṉṟum, vaṭamoḻit tuṇaiyiṉṟi iyaṅkātu eṉṟum, ulaka moḻikaḷuk kellām tāymoḻi vaṭamoḻiyē eṉṟum  tamiḻk kalvi valla peṟṟiyarum muṟṟum mayaṅki yuraikka,
vaṭamoḻi ulaka moḻikaḷukkut tāymoḻi eṉṟāl, ataṟkum maṟṟai moḻikaḷukkum tantai moḻi tamiḻē eṉṟu kūṟiya vaṭalūr aṭikaḷār pukaḻparappāḷarāyp potunilaik kaḻakam kaṇṭa tava aṭikaḷār, taṉittamiḻt tantai eṉṉum perumai peṟṟa maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār āvar.
civaṉiyam cārnta perumakkaḷum, pēraṟivarkaḷum kūṭa, oppārāy, `taṉittamiḻ (taṉittu amiḻ) eṉṟu paḻikkavum iḻikkavum, tuṇintu niṉṟu, avar veḷka; veḷḷiṭai malaiyākat `taṉittamiḻ eṉpatai nilai nāṭṭiya niṟaimalai, maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷārē!
civaṉiya maṭattiṉarum, vaṭamoḻi vallārum, `tol kāppiyak  kaṭal eṉap pukaḻappaṭṭārumām cāmināta tēcikar, nāṇamiṉṟi, 
ainteḻuttāl orupāṭai eṉṟu aṟaiyavē nāṇuvar
aṟivuṭaiyōrē; yāmum atuvē
eṉṟum,
oṉṟē āyiṉum taṉittamiḻ uṇṭō?
eṉṟum - eḻutip parappiṉārāka,
oru col tāṉum vēṟṟuc col viravā nūl yām ceyvēm eṉṟu aimpāṉ nāṉku nūlkaḷ kalaimali pēḻaiyāy eḻutik kāṭcip paṭuttiya māṭciyar maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār!
tolkāppiyak kāval
tamiḻ moḻikku vāytta tolkāppiya vaḷanūl,
vaṭacoṟ kiḷavi vaṭaveḻut torīi
eḻuttoṭu puṇarnta collā kummē	(884)
eṉṟum,
moḻi peyarppuc ceytalilum muṟaimai pēṇa vēṇṭum eṉpatai,
moḻipeyarttu atarppaṭa yāttal	(1597)
eṉṟum,
pēccu vaḻakkil oru kāl vēṟṟuccol vantu viṭiṉum,
marapunilai tirital ceyyuṭ killai 	(1590)
eṉṟum - varampāṇai ceytum, ac cevviyal vaḻittaṭam citaintum cīraḻintum, cēṟum cakatiyum nāṟum ciṟumaiyum kuṟumaiyum vaḻivaḻiyē eyta mūvāyiram āṇṭukaḷāy, ayalōr cūḻcciyālum tiṟavōr mayakkālum ēṟpaṭṭa aḻipāṭukaḷai ellām tāmē ōr iyakkamāy iruntu, viḻippum eḻucciyum ūṭṭiyavar maṟaimalai aṭikaḷē eṉpatāl,
āriyattiṉiṉṟu tamiḻai mīṭṭeṭuppa taṟkāka arum pāṭupaṭṭu ilakkiya ilakkaṇa marapōṭu yāṉ kaṟṟa moḻikaḷ muppatu eṉṉum pāvāṇar,
mūvā yiramāṇṭu mōtum vaṭamoḻiyāl
 cāvām takainiṉṟa taṇṭamiḻai - mēvāk
 kumarit taṉinilaikkuk koṇṭuvanta  appar
 amarar maṟaimalai yār
eṉap pāṭip paraviṉār. `taṉit tamiḻt tantaiyār eṉṟa pārāṭṭum ip pāṭṭiṉ talaippil vaḻaṅkiṉār.
nuṇṇiya nūlpalavum kaṟpatē nōṉpāka
 eṇṇiya vāḻnāḷ elāmaḷittup - paṇṇiya
 centamiḻnūl ovvoṉṟum ceppum kaṭalaṟivai
 paintamiḻ kākkum paṭai
eṉa aṭikaḷ kalvi mēmpāṭṭaiyum kalait tiṟap paṭaippukkaḷiṉ mēmpāṭṭaiyum pāvēntar pāratitācaṉār pārāṭṭukiṟār.
paltuṟai āṟṟal
aṭikaḷiṉ paltuṟai āṟṟalai 1. pērāciriyar, 2. perum pulavar, 3. pāvalar, 4. ārāycciyāḷar, 5. mummoḻip pulavar, 6. moḻipeyarppāḷar, 7. coṟpoḻi vāḷar, 8. eḻuttāḷar, 9. palkalaic celvar, 10. taṉittamiḻt tantaiyār eṉṟu pāvāṇar pakuttuk kāṭṭukiṟār.
mēlum avar tam nuṇmāṇ nuḻaipulattai
paṉimalai mukaṭṭiṉ uyarvum, nīla āṟṟiṉ nīḷamum amaiti vāriyiṉ āḻamum amaintavar maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ eṉkiṟār. (paṉimalai  - imayam; nīla āṟu - nail; amaitivāri - pacipikku mākaṭal).
aṭikaḷār nūlkaḷ
aṭikaḷ paṉmāṇ māttamiḻ nūlkaḷai, nūlaka iyakkat tantai araṅka nātarum, innāḷ caivacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻaka āṭciyarum annāḷ nūlakarumākiya tirumiku irā. muttuk kumāracāmi avarkaḷum 14 vakaimaippaṭutti vaittamai perum pārāṭṭukku uriyatām avai,
1. maruttuvam  2. maṟaiporuḷ  3. ilakkiyam 4. itaḻkaḷ  5. caṅka  ilakkiyam  6. pāṭal 7. nāṭakam  8. putiṉam 9. kaṭitam 10. kaṭṭurai 11. camayam  12. tattuvam 13. varalāṟu 14. camūka iyal eṉpaṉa.
mutaṟ kuṟaḷ vāta nirākaraṇam eṉṉum maṟuppu nūlē, aṭikaḷiṉ mutal nūlāka veḷippaṭukiṉṟatu (1898) annūṉ maṟuppu nayamē nākai aṭikaḷai - pallavapura aṭikaḷāka -taṉittamiḻ aṭikaḷāka ākka arum perum tūṇṭal āyiṟṟām.
aṭikaḷār varalāṟu: kīḻkaṭal cār nākappaṭṭiṉattaic cārnta ciṟṟūr kāṭampāṭi, āṅku vāḻnta aruntavap peruntakaiyar cokkanātar - ciṉṉammaiyār! ivviṇaiyar koṭaiyāyt tamiḻaṉṉai ceyta tavap pēṟṟāl piṟantavar vētācalam. piṟanta peru maṅkala nāḷ ki.pi. 15.07.1876.
cokkanātar, cittamalam aṟuvitta cittamaruttuvar; aṟiva maruttuvam eṉpatum atu. attoḻil avarkku vaḷattai vaḻaṅkiyatu. ataṉāl vētācalam kuṟaivaṟa vāḻntār.
nākappaṭṭiṉattu veciliyaṉ micaṉ paḷḷiyil aintu vakuppuvarai payiṉṟār; ciṟanta māṇavarāka viḷaṅkiṉār. āṟām vakuppil cērum nilaiyil, arumait tantaiyār iyaṟkai eytiṉār. āyiṉum aṉṉaiyār uṟutiyāl kalviyait toṭarntār. appaḷḷiyil āṅkilak kalvikku irunta mutaṉmai tamiḻukku illai. kiṟittava camayam paṟṟi aṉṟic civaṉiyam paṟṟi aṟiya vāyppillai eṉṉum iraṇṭu kuṟaikaḷaiyum iḷamaiyilēyē uṇarntār.
vīṭṭilēyē tamiḻ nūlkaḷaip payiṉṟu mutaṟ kuṟaiyaiyum, nākaiyil irunta intumatāpimāṉa caṅkam vaḻi iraṇṭām kuṟaiyaiyum nīkkiṉār. paḷḷiyilum kuṟaiyaṟak kaṟṟār. varuvāyk kuṟaiyāl vāṭum aṉṉaikkuc cumaiyāy illāmal taṉiyē payilvatē iṉiyavaḻi eṉa eṇṇi, nākaiyil puttaka vaṇikarāka viḷaṅkiya nārāyaṇa cāmiyār pulamaiyālum ciṟantu viḷaṅkiyavar. ātalāl avariṭam taṉiyē ilakkiya  - ilakkaṇa - camaya nūlkaḷaik kaṟṟār. nārāyaṇariṉ muṉṉai māṇavar pērā. maṉōṉmaṇīyam pe. cuntaraṉār eṉpataiyum aṟintār. oṉpatām vakuppoṭu paḷḷik kalviyai niṟuttit taṉikkalviyē kaṟṟār. tolkāppiyam, tokai nūlkaḷ, tirukkuṟaḷ, tirucciṟṟampalak kōvai, iṟaiyaṉār akap poruḷ eṉakkaṟṟār.
aṭikaḷār iyaṟṟiya tiruvoṟṟi murukar mummaṇik kōvai mukappil, ematu patiṉaintām āṇṭil muṟaippaṭi tuvaṅkiya tamiḻp payiṟciyāṉatu ematu irupattōrām āṇṭiṟkuḷ tolkāppiyam, tirukkuṟaḷ, ciṟṟampalak kōvaiyār eṉṉum mūṉṟu nūlkaḷaiyum muḻutum neṭṭuruc ceytu muṭittēm eṉṟum vēṟucila nūlkaḷaiyum muḻuvatāka ōtiyataiyum kūṟi,
iṅṅaṉamāka, viḻumiya tamiḻppaḻa nūlkaḷil ematu karuttu īrppuṇṭu niṉṟu payiṉṟa payiṟciyiṉālēyē ceyyuḷum uraiyum taṉic centamiḻ naṭaiyil eḻutum tiṟam ematu iḷamaip paruvattilēyē emakku vāyppa tāyiṟṟu eṉkiṟār. avvāṇṭu ki.pi. 1897. nākai intu matāpimāṉa caṅkattiṟkuc caiva cittānta caṇṭa marutam eṉṉum virutu peṟṟa cōmacuntara nāyakar vantu coṟpoḻivāṟṟiṉār. avar poḻivil vanta karuttoṉṟaṉai maṟuttu oruvar eḻuta, ataṟku maṟuppāka murukavēḷ eṉṉum puṉaipeyaril maṟuppurai eḻutiṉār. ataṉaikkaṟṟa nāyakar, mīḷap poḻivukku varuṅkāl muruka vēḷaic cantikka virumpiṉār. accantippiṉ payaṉē uṉṉai viraivilē ceṉṉaikku varuvippōm. nī antap pakkaṅkaḷil iruppatē nallatu eṉṟu kūṟiṉār. ituvē aṭikaḷārkkuc ceṉṉai vāḻvu amaiya mutal tūṇṭal āyiṟṟu. aṭikaḷukkup patiṉēḻām vayatil (1893) tirumaṇam nikaḻntatu. tuṇaivi tam māmaṉ makaḷ cavuntara valli eṉpār.
tiruvaṉantapuram
nārāyaṇacāmi avarkaḷiṉ muṉṉai māṇavarām pērā. cuntaraṉār tiruvaṉanta purattil iruntār. avariyaṟṟiya maṉōṉmaṇīya nāṭaka nūl kaṟṟu ataṉ arumai kaṇṭu, pērāciriyaraik kāṇavirumpiṉār. ataṉāl tam āciriyarōṭu tiruvaṉantapuram ceṉṟu pērāciriyarōṭu uraiyāṭiṉār. vētācalaṉār iḷam paruvattaiyum mūtaṟivaiyum aṟinta cuntaraṉār viyappuṟṟut takutic cāṉṟu oṉṟu vaḻaṅkiṉār. ciṟitu kālam tiruvaṉanta purattil āciriyap paṇiyum ceytār. avviṭattiṉ paruvanilai ovvāmaiyāl nākaikkē tirumpiṉār.
ceṉṉai: cōmacuntaraṉār muṉṉē kūṟiyapaṭi vētācalaraic ceṉṉaikku aḻaittu aṟiñar nallucāmi eṉpār naṭāttiya cittānta tīpikai eṉṉum itaḻāciriyar ākkiṉār. piṉṉē ceṉṉai kiṟittavak kallūrikku oru tamiḻp pērāciriyar vēṇṭiyiruttal aṟinta cōmacuntaraṉār vētācalaṉārai akkallūrip pērāciriyarākka muyaṉṟār. atukāl tamiḻttuṟait talaivarāka iruntavar vi.kō cūriyanārāyaṇa cāttiriyār eṉṉum paritimāl kalaiñar. kallūri mutalvar villiyam millar eṉpār.
9.3.1898 il vētācalaṉār paṇiyēṟṟār. aṅkup patiṉmūṉṟu āṇṭukaḷ paṇiyāṟṟiyuḷḷār.
akkallūrik kālattē iḷaṅkalai māṇavarkkup pāṭamāka irunta mullaippāṭṭu (1903) paṭṭiṉappālai (1906) eṉpavaṟṟukku ārāycci urai eḻutiṉār.
avvurai nūlai vāṅkik kaṟṟa māṇākkarum ēṉait tamiḻaṟiñarum putumuṟaiyāka eḻutappaṭṭa avvuraip pāṅkiṉaip piṉpaṟṟip paḻaiya tamiḻ nūlkaḷai ārāyvatil kiḷarcci peṟalāṉār. ipputumuṟai urai, tamiḻ nūlārāyum vakaikaḷai iṉitu viḷakki aṟiñar palarkkum aka makiḻcci yiṉai viḷaittamai kaṇṭu iṉi, ittakaiya uraikaḷaiyē eḻututal vēṇṭum eṉa ematuḷḷam peritum viḻaintatu eṉkiṟār.
ceṉṉaip palkalaik kaḻakattārāṅkilamum vaṭa moḻiyum kaṭṭāya mākap paṭittal vēṇṭum eṉṟum, tāymoḻip pāṭattai viruppap pāṭamākak kaṟpikkalām eṉṟum oru tīrmāṉam āṭcikkuḻuvil koṇṭu vantaṉar. oru pāṭam viruppappāṭam eṉappaṭṭa piṉṉar ataṉaik kaṟka varuvār evar? tamiḻ kaṟkum māṇavar eṇṇikkai kuṟaiyavē āciriyap paṇiyiṭamum kuṟaiya vēṇṭuvatāki, aṭikaḷār 31.1. 1911 ām nāḷuṭaṉ kallūrip paṇiyai muṭikka vēṇṭiya tāyiṟṟu.
māṇavar: ikkālattil aṭikaḷiṉ māṇavarāka iruntāruḷ, taṇikaimaṇi ceṅkalvarāyar, iracikamaṇi ṭi.kē.ci, nāvalar ca.cō. pārati, pēraṟiñar ca. vaiyāpuriyār mutalōr cilar.
taṇikaimaṇiyiṉ, tēvāra tiruvācakac collaṭaivu oru piṟappaḷavil ceyyak kūṭiyatā?
nāvalar ca. cō. pāratiyāriṉ āyvut tuṇivu ettaṉai kacaṭukaḷai nīkkiyatu? vaiyāpuriyār, kāla āyvilum, vaṭamoḻip piṭivātattilum tammaic cālpu āyvil iruntu vilakkiṉālum, caṅka ilakkiyap patippu, nikaṇṭuppatippu, palkalaik kaḻaka akaramutalip patippu ivaṟṟaic ceyta arumai eṉṉa? puṟattiraṭṭu, kaḷaviyal, kārikai āyavaṟṟiṉ patippuc ciṟappu eṉṉa?
ivarkaḷ paṇiyum aṭikaḷārait taḻuvutal uṇmai tāṉē!
camaraca caṉmārkkam
kallūrip paṇiyil iruntu viṭupeṟṟa aṭikaḷār, pallavapurattai vāḻviṭamākak koṇṭār. camaraca caṉmārkka nilaiyam tōṟṟuvittār. 
ellāc cātiyārkkum ellāc camayattārkkum aṅkīkāramāṉa cīvakāruṇiya oḻukkattaiyum, camaraca caṉmārk kattaiyum piṟkālattil mika valiyuṟuttip pōtitta āciriyar srīmat irāmaliṅka cuvāmikaḷē āvār. accuvāmikaḷ valiyuṟuttiya avviraṇṭu koḷkaikaḷaiyum eṅkum valiyuṟutti viḷakki aṉparkaḷai oruṅku kūṭṭa vēṇṭi accuvāmikaḷ iṭṭa peyarālēyē, `camaraca caṉmārkka nilaiyam eṉṉum itu tāpikkalāyiṟṟu. innilaiyattiṟku muṉ āciriyar tiruvaḷḷuva nāyaṉār; piṉ āciriyar irāmaliṅka cuvāmikaḷ āvar eṉṟu peyarīṭu ceytu koḷkaikaḷaiyum vakukkiṟār.
kuṭumpam
1898il aṭikaḷār ceṉṉaikku varumpōtu avartam aṉṉaiyār ciṉṉammaiyār, tuṇaiviyār cavuntiram ammai yār, celvi cintāmaṇi, aṭikaḷār eṉṉum nālvaraik koṇṭa kuṭumpamāka iruntatu.
1911il pallava purattil kuṭipukum pōtu nīlāmpikai, tiruñāṉa campantam, māṇikkavācakam, tirunāvukkaracu, cuntara mūrtti eṉṉum makkaḷum koṇṭa oṉpatiṉmar kuṭumpamāka iruntatu. pallavuram pukunta piṉ tiripura cuntari piṟakkap patiṉmaṉ kuṭumpam āyiṟṟu. innilaiyilēyē 27.08.1911il caivat tuṟaviyarkkuriya tuṟavāṭai (kāvi) koṇṭu cuvāmi vētācalam āṉār. cuvāmi vētācalam eṉpataṉ taṉittamiḻākkamē maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ eṉpatu veḷippaṭai. muṉṉēyē avarai aṭikaḷ eṉṟatu avartam māṇpu karutiyēyām.
mīḷpārvai
nām mīḷpārvai oṉṟupārkka vēṇṭum.
aṭikaḷ 21ām akavaiyilēyē taṉittamiḻ āyvuttiṟam kaivarap peṟṟār.
1903, 1907 ākiya āṇṭukaḷil mullaippāṭṭu, paṭṭiṉappālai ākiya nūlkaḷukku āyvurai vaḻaṅkuṅkāl itiluḷḷa coṟkaḷ ittaṉai; ivaṟṟuḷ centamiḻccol ittaṉai; vēṟṟuc col ittaṉai - eṉa aṟutiyiṭukiṟār.
ātalāl, taṉittamiḻ eṉṉum tōṟṟam 1916 ām āṇṭil nīlāmpikaiyār tūṇṭalāl ēṟpaṭṭamai eṉpatu, akattē amaintu kiṭanta vittu, muḷai viṭṭu veḷippaṭṭa nilaiyām. av veḷippaṭṭa nilaiyum uṭaṉē paṟṟip paṭaravillai! mēlum cila āṇṭukaḷiṉ piṉṉarēyē camacara caṉmārkka nilaiyam, potunilaik kaḻakamākavum, ñāṉacākaram aṟivuk kaṭalākavum, cuvāmi vētācalam maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ ākavum paṭippaṭiyē kiḷarkiṉṟaṉavām!
ōr ariya amaippu uruvākum nilaiyum, ōr ariya kaṇṭu piṭippu ulakavar aṟiya veḷippaṭalum, avai iyakkamākavum payaṉākavum ākiṉṟa nilaiyum paṟṟiya viḷakkam, aṭikaḷār taṉittamiḻ iyakka varalāṟṟil aṭaṅkiyuḷḷatām!
etiriṭait tūṇṭal: mēlum oṉṟu: oru nēriṭait tūṇṭaliṉum etiriṭait tūṇṭal valimai mikkatu eṉpatum, uḷḷattu uṟutiyai veḷḷap perukkāy veḷippaṭutta vallatu eṉpatum teḷivām.
oṉṟē āyiṉum taṉittamiḻ uṇṭā? eṉṟu kaṟṟaṟinta tamiḻar oruvarē viṉāvi nakaittal, peṟṟa tāy moḻip piṟavikku uṟṟa takaimai ākātu eṉpatum, ataṉai uṇarnta appiṟavi pāmpaṉ kurupāta aṭikaḷ pōl cēntaṉ centamiḻ eṉṉa oru nūliyaṟṟit tamiḻ taṉittu iyaṅkavalla taṉi uyar moḻi eṉṟu nilaināṭṭiyiruppiṉ vaḻipaṭattakka piṟaviyāy amaintirukkum! appēṟu ataṟku vāykkāmai,
tuṟantārkkut tuppuravu vēṇṭi maṟantārkol 
maṟṟai yavarkaḷ tavam	(kuṟaḷ 263)
eṉṉum vaḷḷuvar vāy moḻippaṭi,
taṉittamiḻt tantaip pēṟu maṟaimalaiyaṭikaṭku vara iruppataṟkē nēriṭṭa nērcciyām eṉka.
iyakkam
aṭikaḷ iyakkam ālamarameṉak kālpala koṇṭu akala akala viriyat taṉittamiḻ iyakkamāy, ulakattamiḻk kaḻakamāy, pāvalarēṟu tamiḻk kaḷamāy, cērar koṟṟamāy, tamiḻkkāppuk kaḻakamāy, tamiḻp pātukāppuk kaḻakamāy, taṉittamiḻ ilakkiyak kaḻakamāy, tamiḻ vaḷarccik kaḻakamāy, maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ maṉṟamāy, tamiḻkkāval kuḻuvāy, iyaṟṟamiḻp payiṟṟakamāy, pāvāṇar tamiḻ iyakkamāy āṅkāṅku mukiḻttu muṉaippoṭu kaṭaṉāṟṟa ēviyatām.
nūlakam
aṭikaḷ nūṟṟokai, arumaṇik kuvaiyaṉṉa nālāyiram nūlkaḷaik koṇṭatu. etiratu uṇarntu, caivacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakattiṟku muṟkoṭaiyāka vaḻaṅkiyatāl, nālāyiram nāṟpatāyiramāy mēlum mēlum virintu maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ nūlakap peyarāl, perumaimiku vaḷḷalār mutaṟkaṇ poḻivu ceyta periyavar cōmu avarkaḷ illamē vāykka ulaka vaḷamākkiyatām! ataṉ vaḻiyākavē aṭikaḷ tāmē veḷiyiṭṭa nūlkaḷum, veḷiyiṭāmal kaḻaka vaḻi veḷivanta nūlkaḷum, aṭikaḷ kālattiṟkup piṉ veḷivanta nūlkaḷum ākiya muḻumait tokutikaḷum ki.pi. 1898 mutal 1969 varai mutaṟpatippāka veḷivantavai. avaṟṟuḷ paṭikaḷ kiṭṭutaṟku ariyavaiyum uṇṭu. aṭikaḷ nūlkaḷ nāṭṭuṭaimai ākkappaṭṭa pēṟṟāl, iṉṟu vāytta nūlkaḷ aṉaittaiyum oṭṭumottamāka vaḻaṅkum peruvāyppait tamiḻmaṇ patippakam peṟṟatām.
veḷḷac cētam
aṇmaiyil ēṟpaṭṭa veḷḷap perukku patippakattuḷ pukuntu accu aṭikkappaṭṭatum, aṭikkappaṭa iruntatum, kaṇiṉippaṭutti vaitta kuṟuvaṭṭukaḷum,  mūlanūlkaḷum eṉa vāri aḷḷik koṇṭu pōṉamai, kaḷattukku vanta katir maṇikaḷ, kaḷañciyattiṟku vārāmal pōya, pēraḻivu oppatām. ātalāl, nūlkaḷai mīḷat tēṭavum, kaṇiṉippaṭuttavum, kuṟuvaṭṭu ākki acciṭavum eṉa mummaṭaṅkup paṇiyum pēriḻappum perutta avalamum uṇṭākkiṟṟām. annilaiyil,
ūḻaiyum uppakkam kāṇpar ulaiviṉṟit
tāḻā tuñaṟṟu pavar
eṉṉum vāymoḻiyai vāymai moḻiyākkiya tuṇivuc celvar iḷavaḻakaṉārum avar kuṭumpattavarum āvar!
muṉṉaṟivipput tiṭṭappaṭi nūlukku ārvamāyp paṇam celuttiyōrkkum nūl kālattil kiṭṭā uḷaiccal illāmal pōkātē! avarkaḷum amaintu tāṅkal, iyaṟkai iyaṅkiyal vaḻiyatām.
veḷiyīṭukaḷ
tamiḻmaṇ patippakam tamiḻmoḻi iṉa maṇvaḷam - vaḷarcci mīṭṭeṭuppu - eṉpavaṟṟai muṉvaittut tamiḻp pōrāḷi iḷavaḻakaṉārāl toṭaṅkappaṭṭatāl, pēriḻappukkām eṉat teḷivākat terintum, icaik kalaikku vāytta oppaṟṟa kaḷañciyamāy vāytta - karuṇāmirta cākarattai veḷiyiṭṭatām! muṉṉai vanta mutaṟpakuti pēriḻappākkiyum ataṉ piṉṉaip pakutikaḷaiyum aritiṉ muyaṉṟu veḷiyiṭṭatu! moḻiñāyiṟu pāvāṇar; īḻattu aṟiñar na.ci. kantaiyā;  varalāṟṟaṟiñar cāmināta carmā, uraivēntar auvai cu.tu; nāvalar na.mu.vē; paṇṭita vittuvāṉ ti.vē. kōpālar; cāmi citamparaṉār; mayilai cīṉi. vēṅkaṭa cāmiyār; pēraṟiñar ka. veḷḷai vāraṇaṉār; pāvalar kuḻantaiyār; pāvalar muṭiyaracaṉār; pāvēntar, tol poruḷ kalveṭṭu āyñar catācivaṉār, cāttaṉkuḷam irākavaṉ  nūlkaḷaiyum eḷiyēṉ nūlkaḷaiyum muḻumai muḻumaiyāka veḷiyiṭṭatu.
tolkāppiyam- pāṭṭu - tokai - kāppiyam - kīḻkkaṇakku - tamiḻnāṭṭu varalāṟu - āṅkilam valla aṟiñar irāma nātaṉār nūlkaḷai yum, yāḻppāṇa akarāti mutalām akarātit tokutikaḷaiyum veḷi yiṭṭatu. patiṉeṭṭām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ piṟpakutitoṭṭu, irupattōrām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ muṟpakuti varai veḷivanta- miḻukku ākkamām nāṭṭu ṭaimai ākkappaṭṭa nūlkaḷai ellām-ōriṭattup peṟṟu, oru taṉit tamiḻ nūlakam amaikkum aḷavukku acciṭṭu vaḻaṅkiya perumai iḷavaḻakaṉārkku uṇṭu. avarē aṉṟi avar tuṇaiyaiyum makkaḷaiyum uṟavaiyum aṉpaiyum naṇpaiyum oruṅkē iṇaittu, aṟiñar perumakkaḷ palar aravaṇaippaiyum vāyppak koṇṭu vāḻum iyalpu avarkku vāyttalāl maṭṭumē, ivvāṟu ceyaṟkariya ceyya vāyttatām eṉpataṟku eṭuttukkāṭṭākat tikaḻpavarum avar.
maṟaimalaiya māṇpu
maṟaimalaiyam, taṉittamiḻ iyakkam! tamiḻt taṉip peruvaḷam! muttamiḻc curappu! cīrtiruttac ceḻumai! vāḻviyal vaḷam! kalaic collākkak kaḷañciyam! paṇṭait tamiḻar varalāṟṟup putaiyal! āyviyal vaḻikāṭṭi! poḻivup polivu! eḻuttiṉ eḻil! moḻik kāval muṉaiyam! muṭṭaṟukkum muḻutuṟu koṭai! mīṭṭeṭukkum mētakaimai! aḷḷa aḷḷak kuṟaiyā aṇimaṇik kuviyal! eṉal meymai viḷakkamām!
puttakam ēṟṟup polivatē puttakam
 vāḻiya nalaṉē! vāḻiya nilaṉē!
tiruvaḷḷuvar nilaiyam,	iṉpa aṉpuṭaṉ
7, irāmaṉ teru,	irā. iḷaṅkumaraṉ
tirunakar, maturai - 6
625006.

malaikka vaikkum maṟaimalaiyam!
patippurai
moḻiyē iṉamakkaḷ muṉṉēṟa nalla
vaḻiyām ataṉai vaḷarkka -paḻiyāyt
tamiḻayarat tāḻntāṉ tamiḻaṉ avaṉē
tamiḻuyarat tāṉuyarvāṉ tāṉ
moḻippēraṟiñar pāvāṇar avarkaḷ `moḻiyiṉ vaḻiyākavē moḻi pēcum makkaḷ muṉṉēṟa muṭiyum eṉak kūṟukiṟār. ulakam muḻutum iv vuṇmai uṇarappaṭṭuḷḷatai varalāṟu kāṭṭukiṟatu.
pāvāṇarai aṟivulakam cariyāka aṭaiyāḷam kāṇpataṟkuk kāraṇamāṉatu maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār tanta pārāṭṭu!
maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ (15.7.1876 - 15.9.1950) tēvanēyap pāvāṇar (7.2.1902 - 16.1.1981) iruvarum taṉittamiḻiyakkattait tāṅki niṟkum tūṇkaḷākap pōṟṟappaṭupavarkaḷ.
maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār 1916il tōṟṟuvitta taṉittamiḻ iyakkam, nūṟṟāṇṭai eṭṭiyuḷḷa makiḻvāṉa nēram itu!
taṉittamiḻiyakka nūṟṟāṇṭai niṉaivu kūrum vakaiyil, taṉittamiḻiyakkat tantai maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷāriṉ eḻuttu, coṟpoḻivu, nāṭ kuṟippu, kaṭitaṅkaḷ aṉaittaiyum `maṟaimalaiyam eṉum perun tokuppākat tamiḻmaṇ patippakam vaḻi veḷikkoṇarat tiṭṭamiṭṭōm.
ulakam muḻutum paravi vāḻum uṇarvu kuṉṟāta tamiḻarkaḷ tanta ūkkuvippu, intap peruntiṭṭattai niṟaivēṟṟit tīravēṇṭum eṉum uṟutiyai eṉakkut tantatu.
taṉittamiḻ iyakka aṟiñarkaḷum ārvalarkaḷum aṭikaḷāriṉ ariya paṭaippukaḷ palavaṟṟaiyum tēṭittēṭi vaḻaṅki vaḻikāṭṭiṉar; valimaiyūṭṭiṉar.
maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār 1898ām āṇṭiliruntu eḻuti vantuḷḷār. aṟivuk kaṭal (ñāṉacākaram) itaḻai 1902ām āṇṭiliruntu veḷiyiṭṭu vantuḷḷār. avariṉ paṭaippukaḷ peruveḷḷamāy veḷivantirukkiṉṟaṉa.
accēṟātavai, peyar kūṭak kēṭṭaṟiyātavai, peyar terintum pār vaikkuk kiṭṭātavai - eṉap palavakaiyāyk kiṭaittaṉa aṭikaḷāriṉ arum paṭaippukaḷ! avaṟṟai `maṟaimalaiyam eṉum talaippil 34 tokuppukaḷāka orucērap pārkkumpōtu, tamiḻulakam viyappaṭaivatu uṟuti.
moḻinalam iṉanalam pēṇutal aṉṟō
muḻunalam peṟuvaḻi! nāmpeṟal eṉṟō?
ippaṭik kavalaiyōṭu kēṭpār pāvalarēṟu peruñ cittiraṉār (10.3.1933 - 11.6.1995). avariṉ `teṉmoḻi itaḻ 1.8.1959 iliruntu veḷivarat toṭaṅkiya piṉputāṉ, taṉittamiḻt ticai nōkki nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉōr naṭaipōṭat toṭaṅkiṉar. atil nāṉum oruvaṉ.
peruñcittiraṉāriṉ eḻuttu kāṭṭiya aṭaiyāḷattāl, pāvāṇar nūlkaḷ eṉum aṟivupputaiyal palar kaikaḷilum kuṭiyēṟiyatu.
pāvāṇar nūlkaḷ valiyuṟuttip pēciyatāl, maṟaimalai yaṭikaḷārait tēṭippayilum ārvam tamiḻulakil puttuyir peṟṟatu.
āriyattai etirppatilum camaṟkirutattai vilakku vatilumē tamiḻiṉa mēmpāṭum tamiḻk kāppum aṭaṅkiyirukkiṟatu. avvuṇarcciyait tamiḻar neñcaṅkaḷil arumpa vaittatu tirāviṭar iyakkam; malarntu viriyavaittatu taṉittamiḻ iyakkam! kumuka, araciyal, paṇpāṭṭu malarccikku inta īriyakkaṅkaḷiṉ pēruḻaippup payaṉpaṭṭatu.
tamiḻakattil taṉittamiḻ uṇarvu ki.mu. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉ taḷarat toṭaṅkiyatu; 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉ cariyat toṭaṅkiyatu. vaṭamoḻi veḷḷattil tamiḻ iḻuttuc cellappaṭṭu viṭāmal kāppāṟṟiyōr kamparum pavaṇantiyum! piṉvantōrukku antak kāppuṇarcci illāmal, ayaṉ moḻip paṟṟukku aṭimaiyāṉatāl tamiḻum tamiḻiṉamum peṟṟa iḻappukaḷ ērāḷam!
ayaṉmoḻic cēṟṟiliruntu tamiḻai mīṭkum muyaṟci āṅkilēyar varukaiyaiyoṭṭip puttuyir peṟṟatu. vaḷḷalār, pāmpaṉ cāmikaḷ, vaṇṇac carapam taṇṭapāṇi cuvāmikaḷ, cuppiramaṇiyacivā, paritimāṟ kalaiñar mutaliya palarum toṭakka vitaikaḷāyiṉar.
kōvai avināciyil piṟantu virutunakaril vāḻnta virutai civañāṉayōki 19.11.1908 ām nāḷ `tiruviṭar kaḻakam toṭaṅkit taṉittamiḻ uṇarccikku vēkaṅkūṭṭiṉār. avvamaippil uṟuppiṉarāyiruntavar cuvāmi vētācalam (malaimalaiyaṭikaḷ).
maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ eṉat tam peyaraik koṇṭu, 1916il avar toṭaṅki yatē taṉittamiḻ iyakkam.
`tuṟaimiku āṅkilam maṟaivaṭa moḻiyoṭu
 tūytamiḻp pēraṟiñar - taṉittamiḻ
 tōṟṟiya ōraṟiñar
pāvalarēṟu peruñcittiraṉār pāṭiyataṟkēṟpa, maṟaimalai yaṭikaḷār tamiḻ, āṅkilam, camaṟkirutam mutaliya paṉmoḻiyilum pulamai mikkavar.
paṉmoḻip pēraṟiñarāṉa maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār tamiḻukkum tamiḻarukkum ātaravāka niṉṟatāl etirppukkum iḻappukkum āḷākkappaṭṭār. āṉālum uṟuti kuṉṟāmal tamiḻukku ātaravāy uḻaikkum uḷḷattōṭiruntār. ataṉāl, tamiḻar uḷḷaṅkaḷil iṉṟaḷavum māṟāp perumaiyōṭu avar vīṟṟirukkiṉṟār.
vāḻttāta nāḷillai vaiyakam
  maṟaimalai yaṭikaḷ maṟaiyāt tiruppeyar
  vāḻttāta nāḷillai vaiyakam
pāvēntar pāratitācaṉ avar māṇpiṉaip pāṭi makiḻntatum, tam `kuṭumpa viḷakku nūliṉai maṟaimalai yaṭikaḷukkuk kāṇikkaiyākki vaḻaṅkiyatum, avariṉ uṟuti taḷarāta uḷḷamum uḻaippum kaṇṭu pōṟṟiya naṉṟik kaṭaṉ eṉpatil ayyamillai.
ceṉṉaiyil kaṭanta irupatu āṇṭukaḷākac ceyalpaṭṭu varum tamiḻmaṇ patippakap paṇikaḷait tamiḻulakam aṟiyum.
vāḻum talaimuṟaikkum varuṅkālat talaimuṟaikkum vaḻikāṭṭavalla tamiḻaṟiñarkaḷ palariṉ nūlkaḷai muḻumuḻut tokuppukaḷāka vaḻaṅkiyiruppatu tamiḻmaṇ patippakattiṉ muṭippiṉai (cātaṉai)yākap pōṟṟappaṭukiṟatu.
tamiḻ maṟumalarccikku vittiṭṭa aṟiñarkaḷaik kaṇṭaṟintu, avarkaḷiṉ nūlkaḷai muḻumaiyāṉa tokuppukaḷākat tamiḻmaṇ patippakam veḷiyiṭṭu varukiṟatu. avai kālam muḻutum payaṉpaṭavalla karuvi nūlkaḷ eṉpatai aṟiñar ulakam aṟiyum!
tamatu nalattai otukki vaittu, tamiḻ nalattaik kākka vāḻnta aṟiñarkaḷiṉ aṉaittu nūlkaḷaiyum oru cērat tokuttu muḻut tokuppāka vaḻaṅkuvatu perumpaṇi! ataṉait tamiḻmaṇ patippakam toṭarntu niṟaivēṟṟi varukiṟatu. tokuppu nūlkaḷiṉ paṭṭiyalaip piṉṉiṇaippil kāṇka.
tamiḻukkāka uḻaikkum uṇarvai iḷamaiyil emmiṭam ēṟpaṭuttiyōruḷ perumpulavar nakkīraṉārum avartam iḷaval pulavar cittiravēlaṉārum kuṟippiṭattakkōr! em naṉṟikkuriyōr!
taṉittamiḻ ārvam, tirāviṭar iyakka īṭupāṭu eṉum iru ciṟakukaḷāl emmai eḻavaittavar perumpulavar nakkīraṉār! avarai eṇṇik kaṇpaṉikka naṉṟi kūṟukiṟatu em neñcam.
kālappaḻamaiyālum parāmarippuk kuṟaivālum aḻiya nērnta tamiḻilakkiyaṅkaḷ aḷavaṟṟavai! avaṟṟil orupakutiyai yāvatu mīṭṭu nam kaikaḷukku vaḻaṅkiyōr āṟumukanāvalar, ci.vai. tāmōtaraṉār, u.vē.cāminātar mutaliya cāṉṟōr perumakkaḷ!
tamiḻilakkiyap peruñcelvattai mīṇṭum mīṇṭum accākki, aṉaivar kaikaḷaiyum eṭṭac ceyta perumai - caivacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakam, u.vē.cā. nūlakam, maṇivācakar patippakam mutaliya patippāḷarkaḷukku uriyatu.
accuttuṟai aṟimukamāṉa piṉpē, aṟivuttuṟai aṉaivarukkum potuccottākum vāyppu vantatu. eṭṭāk kaṉiyāka ōlaic cuvaṭikaḷil muṭaṅkik kiṭanta ilakkiyac celvaṅkaḷ, ellōrukkum eḷitāyk kiṭaikkum paṇiyaic ceyta patippāciriyarkaḷum, patippāḷarkaḷum nam naṉṟik kuriyōr!
iravu pakal pārātu mutumaiyaik karutātu uḻaikkum maṉavurattai muṉṉōṭip patippāciriyarkaḷiṉ paṇiyē emakku vaḻaṅkiyatu.
aracum, palkalaik kaḻakaṅkaḷum, aṟaniṟuvaṉaṅkaḷum, camaya pīṭaṅkaḷum ,perum iyakkaṅkaḷum, perum celvarkaḷum ceyya vēṇṭiya perun tamiḻppaṇi itu! eḷiyavarkaḷāl iyaṅkum tamiḻmaṇ patippakam peruntamiḻp paṇikaḷai iṉṉalkaḷukku iṭaiyil arumuyaṟciyōṭu ceytu varuvatu, uḷḷavuram oṉṟiṉāltāṉ!
vallamai cērkkum valimaiyuṇ ṭākkum!
  vaṇṭamiḻ naintiṭil etunammaik kākkum?
kākkum tamiḻaik kākkum uṇarvaip pāvēntar pāṭal varikaḷ tantaṉa.
accukku eṭṭāmalum kaikkuk kiṭṭāmalum irunta pēraṟiñar palariṉ nūlkaḷai nāṭṭuṭaimaiyākki tirāviṭar iyakka aracukaḷ vaḻaṅkiṉa. ataṉāl, aṟivu paravalākum vāyppukaḷ uruvāyiṉa.
`carivum iḻappum namakku vantālum tamiḻukku varakkūṭātu eṉa eṇṇi uḻaikkum iyakkaṅkaḷum amaippukaḷum tamiḻ nāṭṭil vaṟṟāmal vaḷarntapaṭi irukkiṉṟaṉa.
tiruviṭar kaḻakam (1908)
taṉittamiḻ iyakkam (1916)
teṉṉintiya nalaurimaik kaḻakam (1916)
ceṉṉai mākāṇat tamiḻccaṅkam (1934)
tamiḻppātukāppuk kaḻakam (1937)
tamiḻaṟiñar kaḻakam (1943)
tamiḻp pērāyam (1959)
tamiḻkkāppuk kaḻakam (1961)
taṉittamiḻk kaḻakam (1964)
ulakattamiḻk kaḻakam (1968)
tamiḻiyakkam (1972)
tamiḻc cāṉṟōr pēravai (1998)
tamiḻmalarccikkum taṉittamiḻ vaḷarccikkum aṭittaḷamamaitta ittaku amaippukaḷukkumūlavicaiyākattikaḻumperumaiyaip peṟuvōr maṟaimalai yaṭikaḷārum pāvāṇarumē!tamiḻvaḷattiṟkum tamiḻar nalattiṟkum civaṉiya mēmpāṭṭiṟkum maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār āṟṟiya paṇikaḷ aḷap pariyavai. avai toṭarpāka avar āyntaḷitta aṟivu muttukaḷ eṇṇaṟṟavai!
aṭikaḷāriṉ aṉaittup paṭaippukaḷum poruḷvaḻip pirikkappaṭṭu, kāla varicaiyil 34 tokuppukaḷākat tokukkap paṭṭu `maṟaimalaiyam eṉa uṅkaḷ kaikaḷil ippōtu tavaḻkiṉṟatu.
pala talaimuṟaikaḷukkup payaṉpaṭum aṟivuk karuvūlam itu!
tamiḻar illantōṟum irukkavēṇṭiya aruntamiḻc celvam itu!
aṟivulakattāl `maṟaimalaiyam varavēṟkappaṭum eṉṉum nampikkai emakkuṇṭu. nūṟ curaṅkattil nuḻaiya aḻaikkiṟōm.
taṉakkeṉa vāḻvatu cāvukkoppākum
  tamiḻkkeṉa vāḻvatē vāḻvatākum
eṉum pāvēntar varikaḷai niṉaivuk kūṟukiṟōm.
- kō. iḷavaḻakaṉ

patippāḷar vivaram
kō. iḷavaḻakaṉ 
piṟanta nāḷ		:	3.7.1948
piṟanta ūr		:	uṟantairāyaṉkuṭikkāṭu añcal					urattanāṭu vaṭṭam - 614 625							tañcāvūr māvaṭṭam.
kalvi		:	kallūri pukumuka vakuppu
ippōtaiya toḻil		:	puḻakkattil illāta paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷait 			tēṭiyeṭuttu veḷiyiṭal

āṟṟiyuḷḷa potuppaṇikaḷ
1965il paḷḷi māṇavaṉāka iruntapōtē moḻip pōrāṭṭattil muṉaippākap paṅkēṟṟut taḷaippaṭuttap peṟṟu 48 nāḷkaḷ ciṟaiyil iruntavar.
piṟanta ūrākiya uṟantairāyaṉkuṭikkāṭṭil `ūrnalaṉ vaḷarccik kaḻakam eṉum camūka amaippil iruntu ūr nalappaṇi āṟṟiyavar.
urattanāṭṭil `tamiḻar urimaik kaḻakam eṉṉum amaippaiyum, pāvāṇar paṭippakattaiyum naṇparkaḷuṭaṉ iṇaintu niṟuvit tamiḻmoḻi, tamiḻiṉa, tamiḻaka mēmpāṭṭiṟku uḻaittavar. iḷam talaimuṟaikkut tamiḻt toṇṭāṟṟiyavar.
pēraṟiñar aṇṇāviṉ matuvilakkuk koḷkaiyai neñcil ēnti urattanāṭu matuvilakkuk kuḻuviṉ mukkiya amaippāḷarkaḷil oruvarāka iruntu ceyalāṟṟiyavar. 1975-il tamiḻnāṭu caṭṭamaṉṟattil `urattanāṭu tiṭṭam eṉṟu pārāṭṭap peṟṟa matuvilakkut tiṭṭam veṟṟi peṟa uḻaittavar.
tamiḻmaṇ patippakattai niṟuvi puḻakkattil illāta  paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷaiyum, putiya paṭaippu ilakkiyaṅkaḷaiyum, 19  ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṟutiyilum 20ām nūṟṟāṇṭut toṭakkat tilum vāḻnta tamiḻccāṉṟōrkaḷ eḻuti vaittuc ceṉṟa tamiḻiṉ aṟivuc celvaṅkaḷait tēṭi eṭuttu muḻumaiyākac cērttu poruḷvaḻip pirittu, kāla niralil orē vīccil toṭar toṭarāka  veḷiyiṭṭut tamiḻnūl patippil taṉi muttirai patittu varupavar.
potunilai
taṉittamiḻ iyakkat tantai maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ, tantai periyār, pāvēntar pāratitācaṉ, pēraṟiñar aṇṇā,  moḻinūl mūtaṟiñar ñā. tēvanēyap pāvāṇar,  pāvalarēṟu peruñcittiraṉār ākiyōrai vaḻikāṭṭikaḷākak koṇṭu avartam koḷkai kaḷai niṟaivēṟṟa ayarātu uḻaittu varupavar.

tāḷ vaḻaṅkiyōr:  civā tāḷ maṇṭi, ceṉṉai,

accu maṟṟum nūl kaṭṭamaippu:  						veṅkaṭēcuvarā āpceṭ piriṇṭarcu, pirācacu intiyā						
maṟaimalaiyam ellā nilaiyilum ceppamuṟa veḷi varuvataṟku pallāṟṟāṉum utaviyāka irunta ivarkaḷukku em naṉṟiyum pārāṭṭum.



mutaṟkuṟaḷ vāta nirākaraṇam
mutaṟkuṟaḷakiya,
akara mutala eḻuttellām āti
  pakavaṉ mutaṟṟē ulaku
eṉpatu aṟiñarkaḷ pārvaiyil palatiṟap poruṭ kūṟukaḷukku iṭamāki iṉṟuvarai toṭaravē ceykiṉṟatu.
parimēlaḻakar mutalām uraiyāciriyarkaḷoṭu, putturai kāṇpāraiyum orunilaippaṭāvakaiyil tarukkap poruḷākavē iruntu varukiṟatu.
āti pakavaṉ eṉpatu koṇṭu poruntā iḻimaip puṉaivum ulāvantatu! ammaiyappaṉ eṉavum kāṇappaṭṭatu.
mutaṟkuṟaḷ uvamai eṉappēraṟiñar ku. kōtaṇṭa pāṇiyārāl 200kku mēṟpaṭṭa pakka aḷavil āyvu nūlum veḷippaṭṭatu.
pakavaṉ eṉpatu `pakalavaṉ eṉpataṉ tokai eṉavum palar karutiṉar.
tolkāppiyap pāyira urai pōl mutaṟkuṟaḷum aṟiñarkaḷiṉ tarukkap poruḷāy muṭinta muṭivu eṭṭāmal toṭaravē ceykiṉṟatu. mutaṟkuṟaḷ vāta nirākaraṇam eṉpatu aṭikaḷār kālam varai kāṇappaṭṭa uraikaḷaik kūṟi avaṟṟai maṟuttut tam kōḷ nāṭṭiyatākum. atu veḷippaṭṭa āṇṭu 1898.
- irā. iḷaṅkumaraṉ

tukaḷaṟu  pōtam
meykaṇṭār paramparaiyil vanta cīrkāḻic ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷ eṉpavar iyaṟṟiya nūl itu. ivaraik kāḻi paḻutaikaṭṭi ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷ eṉṟum kūṟuvar.
tukaḷ āvatu mācu malam kuṟṟam eṉappaṭuvatu. maṉamācukaḷil talaippaṭṭatu. āṇavamalam. atu yāṉ eṉatu eṉṉum cerukku ākum. avvāṇavam akalutaṟkuriya aṟivuttiṟattai viḷaṅka uraikkum meyyiyal nūlē tukaḷ aṟu pōtamām.
ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷ pōtaka āciriyarākavum, pōtaka nūlāciriyarākavum tiruttoṇṭu ceyta perumaiyar. caivappayir vaḷartta cāṉṟōrkaḷuḷ oruvar. 14ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉar; cīrkāḻi vāḻviṉar.
kāppuc ceyyuḷuṭaṉ 101 veṇpākkaḷai uṭaiyatu innūl. mupporuḷām iṟai uyir taḷai innūl. mup poruḷām iṟai uyir taḷai eṉpavaṟṟai eḷitil viḷakkum ariya nūl.
ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷiṉ māṇavarkaḷuḷ oruvar tattuvap pirakācar. avar iyaṟṟiya nūl tattuvap pirakācam, tukaḷaṟu pōtak kaṭṭaḷai eṉpavaṟṟaiyum eḻutiṉār. civañāṉa pōtam mutalām nūlkaḷaik kaṟṟut teḷiya vaḻikāṭṭum oḷiviḷakkaṉaiya nūlkaḷ ivai eṉalām. maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷār tukaḷaṟu pōtattiṟku uraikaṇṭu 1898 il patippittār.
- irā. iḷaṅkumaraṉ




poruntum uṇavum poruntā uṇavum

1961il caivacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakam patippitta
nūliṉai mūlamākak koṇṭu innūl patippikkappaṭukiṉṟatu.
innūlaip paṟṟiya kuṟippurai...
makkaḷiṉ nākarika vāḻviṟkum, uṭal tūymaikkum, uḷattūymaikkum, nōyaṟṟa peru vāḻvukkum, aṟivārnta iṉpattiṟkum innūl tuṇai niṟkiṟtu, poruntum uṇavu poruntā uṇavu, kollāmai, pulāl uṇṇāmai caiva uṇavukku māṟāṉa koḷkaikaḷiṉ maṟuppu eṉpaṉa kaṭṭurai amaippukaḷākum.
iyaṟkai vaḻaṅkum uṇavup poruḷkaḷiṉ uyvaṉavukaḷ kēṭuṟāta vakaiyil avaṟṟaip payaṉpaṭutta aṟivuṟuttukiṟār aṭikaḷār. pal vakaip paṇṭaṅkaḷaiyum māṟimāṟi uṭkoṇṭu palvakai uyvaṉavukaḷaip peṟutalum, iṭam kālam uṭaliyalpu ākiyaṉa uṇarntu, cuvaittuṇṭu vāḻntu uḻaippatum nīṭiya nalvāḻvukku vaḻiyākum.
ūṉ uṉpatu uṭal tollaikaḷaip perukkum. payir paccaikaḷaiyum avaṟṟiṉ payaṉkaḷaiyum uṇpatu uṭal nalaṉukku ukantatu. caiva uṇavu koḷḷum iyalpiṉarē iṟai yuṇarviṉ iyaṟkaikku icaintu naṭappavarkaḷ; pulāl uṇpavarkaḷ payaṉpāṭṭaṟivum āṟṟalum peṟār eṉpaṉa aṭikaḷāriṉ uṇavuk koḷkai kaḷām.
- ṭākṭar nā. ceyappirakācu
maṟaimalaiyaṭikaḷ ilakkiyappaṭaippukaḷ (pak. 12)


Preface
 Of the three kingdoms in nature into which the created things are comprehensively classified, man finds his place fixed in the animal kingdom. Though the animal and the vegetable are alike in having, in common, life and growth, the one is not identical with the other but is distinguished from it by possessing the faculty of will potent to perform a complex series of actions for the sake of maintaining its existence and preserving its life from the onslaught of ferocious animals and other individuals of its own class. As for the vegetable naturally no such facilities seem to exist. Pressed by hunger when animals roam about in search of prey, or when a number of them, coming upon something not sufficient for them all to feed upon, engage themselves in a severe conflict over it, or when some of them, too weak and timid to withstand the attack of fierce kinds. flee away for life, one can see what a marvellous and tactful variety of actions denied to plant life they perform.
Since the vegetable kingdom is unlike the animal, in being left unprovided for its defence, it is clear that nature intended her to be used  for food by animals; while on the contrary she has forbidden one animal being preyed upon by another by implanting in each a passionate desire for preserving its life and by giving to each the means of accomplishing it. It may even be imagined that, in the remote past, nature could have made even the wild animals subsist solely upon herbs, fruits and roots and that it is only at a later period when certain large - sized and ferocious types of animals, belonging to polar regions where they had none of the vegetable products to live upon except animal flesh, migrated into genial southern climes, that the habit of preying upon the weaker ones became established in them. Once acquired this cruel habit came to be imitated by the animals of the tropical regions also , until it became a part and parcel of their nature.
But mother Nature never leaves unpunished those of her offspring that go astray from her control and learn to play foul towards her other children. As has been proved by the eminent Dr. A. Haig, all animal matter is saturated with uric acid poison, the fruitful source of numerous virulent types of diseases. Man or beast, all that take to animal diet import into their blood the poison of a very pernicious kind which not only undermines their own health but also tends to deteriorate both the mind and body of their progeny. In this manner all the flesh - eating creatures are made gradually to lose their vitality and become extinct at the end of a few centuries. To this hidden cause that thus saps the essentials of life, must we attribute the extinction of the huge carnivorous animals  which in the past prehistoric ages were roving about the earth in large numbers. Even now how few in number are such harmful and worthsless animals as lions, tigers, bears, wolves and the like when compared with the swarms of such mild strong and useful animals as cattle, horse, sheep, deer and others that live entirely upon leaves and herbs.
Now, a problem of vital importance that concerns the lives of millions every year, remains unnoticed even by men of culture; for the source of terrible epidemics has not as yet been properly inquired into, and the remedial measures that are sought to bring relief to the suffering and the dying multitude do but touch the surface and not the depth of that source. As has been shown above, all animal carcasses being charged with extremely poisonous substance, it is from their exudations that infectious diseases of a terrible kind take their rise, spread rapidly everywhere and carry away millions of lives every year and every season. The disintegrated particles of putrid flesh continually escape into the air, find by this means an easy entrance into the living systems and produce pestilences varying in their action and character and frightful in their consequences. Whether of man or of beast every living flesh is apt to putrefy if not properly guarded against and a constant effusion of poisonous matter fills up the air until it spreads it to an extent of many hundred, nay even thousands of miles, in space. This festering property inherent in flesh requires to be held in check so long as the desire to perpetuate life continues. To do this effectually Nature has áovided us for food and medicine with different kinds of edible plants and fruits which alone are entirely free from uric acid poison. But unfortunately the major portion of mankind and animals of certain kinds have, as their staple food, adopted flesh obtained by the cruel and heartless act of killing the innocent and most useful animals all over the world. Every cruel deed brings with it its own punishment. As pointed out above. Nature has put into the core of all animal matter a dreadfull poison - dreadful alike to those who look into its silent workings and to those who overlook them in their rapacious craving for meat diet which is working out the ruin of all who thwart her benign purposes and violate her natural laws. With the daily consumption of meat - diet, man constantly receives into his blood an infusion of animal poison and thereby arguments the festering process that is already going on within his own flesh.
What a foolish creature man is with all his learning and ingenious inventions to add poison to poison and kill others as well as himself! Instead of taking the wholesome dish of vegetable meal that produces pure, rich and vital blood and preserves flesh from putrefaction, he continually gorges stinking meat, until he falls ill when as a matter of necessity he abstains from the unnatural diet and seeks as a curative means medicines prepared from plants and herbs. Even then the abnormal craving for meat is so strong in many that they entreat their physician to allow them the use of light and easily digestible animal food and prescribe at the same time some powerful portion to assist the digestion of such strong food. And many a physician being more particular about his fees than the health of his patients, dares not lose his professional call but acceds to their entreaties and goes on prescribing medicines one after another along with chicken broth and meat-soup which, containing as they do, a great quantity or urinc acid poison, only tend to increase the gravity of the patients’ sick condition.
In other cases where the patient is a strict vegetarian and the attendant doctor a flesh-eater, the doctor invariably induces the patient to use beef-soup and port wine even against his wish. If, by chance or through some mysterious cause which still reamins unknown to the scientific world, the patient recovers his health, the doctor is congratulated - why he congratulates himself, on the success of his treatment and from that time forward he not only gains the confidence of other suffering people but even believes in his own professional skill to a degree that he makes it a point to extend without any hesitation the benefit of his clever prescription of meat-soup to his other patients also. But if, contrary to his expectations, the patient expires, which occurs in six or seven cases out of ten, he attributes it to the patient’s low vitality or to some trivial mistakes committed either by the helpless man or by his relatives, he himself not knowing that his patient’s death was brought about by the very beef-soup and egg-flip that he prescribed. And strangely enough in the course of a few days following the death of his victim, the doctor takes care to collect his fees from the relatives of the dead man. Such alas! is the doctor’s profession and such the fate of those whoseek his help! And yet, what is the real cause of his death? May it not be the blood-poison augmented to an extent so as to affect the bodily functions by a frequent introduction of more poison in the shape of meat soup and wine into the life-fluid that had already been rendered impure by the disease? Even the lower carnivorous animals, when they are taken ill, abstain from flesh and fast for days together taking at intervals only herbs and roots until they are completely recovered. A careful observer of animal life can never fail to notice this instinctive act on theri part to rid themselves of the accumulation of blood impurities. Even among us the innocent and hard working people who live in villages removed far away from the contamination of town-life, very seldom feel unwell and when they do so they invariably fast until their appetite is improved. In a day or two they get better simply because their chief food consists of light gruel and cheap and wholesome vegetables, animal food being very scarce in rural parts. In this manner their - recovery from any occasional illness is speedy and sure and they live to a good old age.
This simple, harmless, and healthy life led instinctively by country folks and lower animals during the period of illness must have formed a model lesson to the town people who boast of learning and enlightenment, rank and civilization. What availeth all learning, light and rank, if one’s life is rendered unfit for the enjoyment of physical, moral, intellectual and spiritual pleasures and be cut off prematurely by sickness and sorrow! It is, therefore, absolutely necessary that every one should know the proper means of right living and adopt it at any cost. All the physical troubles now existing might be traced to the root cause of blood -poison called otherwise Uric Acid. It is this poison that produces multifarious kinds of diseases by being carried into the vital fluid of the human frame through flesh foods. To avoid blood poison, one must totally avoid meat food which is penetrated with uric acid poison.
Observe too how good, how mild, how useful are those animals that feed upon grass and straw, leaves and roots and how bad, how fierce and how worthless those that live upon flesh food. Do not bulls and cows, horses and camels, sheep and deer live only upon grass, and straw, leaves and reeds rejected by man as useless? And yet, how strong, how clean, and how serviceable they are to man who treats them mercilessly! Can any one make lions, tigers, wolves and panthers as useful, as these? It is impossible and impracticable. Man who has climbed up the ladder of evolutionary process so as to become possessed with a tender heart and a thinking mind which form the foundation of his moral character, should have bestowed his thgought on the intimate relation in which he stands to lower animals which by serving him in many ways, make his existence happy and comfortable and have, therefore, a strong claim on him to be treated kindly and protected from being slaughtered. Dimmed by impetuous passions and strong appetite the mind of carnivorous animals can pay no heed to the value and existence of other lives as they do to their own, but man who has risen far above them in point of culture and refinement of feelings should not have been so carried away by such brutal passions and appetency as to lose sight of the great value which every moving being sets so ardently on its own life and existence.
In what does the excellence of man consist? Is it not in the freedom of his will, in the power he can freely exercise to discriminate between what is good and what is evil? Is it not in the tenacity of his resolve to cling to that alone which brings happiness not only to him but to all moving beings that have a right to live being possessed like him with an instinct of self-preservation?  This right given to every creature not by man but by Providence, this right to live in an organism even the seemingly trivial but truly marvellous stgructure of which the combined intelligence of all the scientific men in the world cannot raise, this right which each and evry moving creature possesses for the purpose of promoting  the growth of its understanding and enlivening its capacity for enjoyment, if man, in his eagerness to fill his stomach, would still refuse to recognize, then the supreme ethical importance that is attached to his existence in the universal conception of the moral law becomes empty and meaningless; for, by this disregard he descends lower down in the scale of culture and delicacy of moral sense and joins the brute creations. It is, therefore, absolutely necessary that, in order to maintain his ethical status and widen the scope it affords him for a freer and fuller play of all his finer sensibilities which not only secure him against the troubles incident to lower forms of lives but also increase the extent and intensity of his happiness, he must totally avoid infringing on the rights of other moving lives and adhere strictly to pure vegetable diet alone.
But it is to be borne in mind that even among the articles of vegetarian diet there are those that contain proportionately a greater quantity of uric acid poison then is found in the meat preparations. Such are tea, coffee, cocoa etc. These and stimulants like alcohol, tobacco, opium and others must be studiously avoided by all who wish to lead a healthy and long life and to produce and perpetuate a long line of descendants strong and sound in both mind and body. Of the substances that compose a wholesome vegetarian meal entirely free from uric acid poison, the following six classes of foodstuffs are mentioned by Dr. A. Haig as sufficient to build up all the constituents of a sound and vigorous human body. They are 1. Milk and its products 2. Bread, cereal foods and gluten 3. Nuts and nut-foods 4. Garden vegetables as potatoes 5. Garden fruits as apples 6. Dried and foreign fruits. In addition to being cheap, these are sweet to taste, sweet to semll, pleasing to mind, harmless and humane and using them as food is in complete harmony with the high ethical station of life to which man has arisen after so many hard and fierce life and death struggles. May it therefore behave every man and every woman to awaken to a sense of the value of their ethical life and lead it so as not to cause any harm either to man or any innocent animal! And be it remembered that there can be no harm greater than that of killing a living being and depriving it of the life given not by man but by providence for the great benefit of improving its understanding by a knowlede and experience of the world!
The above was contributed by me as an article to The Indian Humanitarian magazine conducted by The Bombay Humanitarian League and appeared in the 5th no. of its first volume, in January, 1920. For the benefit of those of our countrymen who wish to read everything in English instead of in their own mothertongue, it is re-published here with many alterations and a few additions. For this I owe my obligation to the above-mentioned League which by publishing the HUmanitarian magazine and numerous books, booklets and pamphlets, has advanced the cause of vegetarianism and brought immense relief to our much useful domestic animals by stopping many animal-sacrifices and converting thousands of our people to vegetarianism. Those who wish to go deeper into this vitally important subject of vegetarianism will do well to go through all the publications of this League and make themselves thoroughly acquainted with the inestimable knowledge they yield of right living.
Now, the Tamil work that appears in the following pages consists of four parts of which the first two are taken from my large Tamil work on ‘Long Life’. The third part which deals with the huimanitarian teachings of the great religions of the world was written many years ago and it appeared in many periodicals that were then in existence. It is now inserted here, so that it may be of permanent use to the Tamil reading public. The fourth and the last part is newly written to meet in a plain scientific spirit the arguments raised against vegetarianism by the adherents of the Tamil atheistic movement that is now set of foot.
Pallavaram									         Vedachalam
 										               9th May 1929.
